    *   Is b7 safe? If c7 move to b7, is it under attack?
    *   If c7 move to c6, is it under attack?

    Let's analyze the Black pieces attacking c6:
    - Black King (e8) is too far.
    - Black Queen (e5) can attack c6? Yes, e5 to c6 is not a legal queen move (that's a knight move). No, e5 to c6 is not possible for a queen.
    - Black Pawn (c5) cannot attack c6 (pawns move forwards).
    - Black Rook (a8) cannot attack c6.
    - Black Rook (f1) cannot attack c6.
    - Black Pawn (a7) cannot attack c6.

    Let's analyze the Black pieces attacking b7:
    - Black Rook (a8) can attack b8, but the pawn is at a7. The rook at a8 can move to b8, but a7 is blocked. Wait, the rook is at a8, can it attack b7? No.
    - Black Queen (e5) can attack b7? (e5 to b8? no; e5 to b5? yes; e5 to b2? yes. What about b7? e5-d6-c7-b8... no). e5-b8? No. e5 to b7? That's a knight move.

    - Black Rook (f1) cannot attack b7.

    If `c7c6` is an option, it captures a piece. In chess, capturing is generally better than not capturing if the position is safe.

    Let's re-verify the board:
    White pieces: King at c7.
    Black pieces: King e8, Rook a8, Rook f1, Queen e5, Pawn a7, Pawn c6, Pawn c5, Pawn f7.

    Current White move options:
    1. `c7b7`
    2. `c7c6` (captures black pawn)

    Since `c7c6` eliminates a piece and keeps the king active, it seems like the superior move.

    Let's double-check checks.
    Is White in check at c7?
    Black pieces:
    - Ra8: cannot hit c7 (a7 is in the way).
    - Ke8: cannot hit c7.
    - Qe5: can it hit c7? Yes! e5 to c7 is a diagonal move. The Queen at e5 is attacking the King at c7.
    So White is currently in check!
    If White is in check, he must move out of check.
    Where is the Queen at e5 attacking?
    Diagonal: e5 -> d6 -> c7. Yes, it's a check.
    Diagonal: e5 -> f6 -> g7 -> h8.
    Diagonal: e5 -> f4 -> g3 -> h2.
    Diagonal: e5 -> d4 -> c3 -> b2 -> a1.

    Now let's see if `c7b7` or `c7c6` gets the king out of check.
    - `c7b7`: b7 is not on the e5-c7 diagonal. Is b7 attacked by any other piece?
        Black Rook a8: No.
        Black King e8: No.
        Black Queen e5: Diagonal from e5 to b7? No (e5 to b8 is not diagonal, e5 to d7 is diagonal, d7 to c7... no). e5 to b7 is a knight move.
        Black Pawn a7: attacks b6.
        Black Pawn c6: attacks b7? Yes! The pawn at c6 attacks b7.
        So `c7b7` moves the king into check from the pawn at c6. (Wait, that would make it an illegal move, but `get_legal_moves` lists it).
        Let's re-check pawn attacks. Pawns attack diagonally *forward*. For black pawns, forward is towards row 1.
        Black pawn at c6: attacks b5 and d5.
        Black pawn at a7: attacks b6.
        Wait, black pawns move from row 7 towards row 1.
        So a pawn at c6 attacks b5 and d5.
        A pawn at a7 attacks b6.
        So the pawn at c6 does NOT attack b7.

    - `c7c6`: captures the pawn at c6.
        Is c6 under attack by other black pieces?
        Black Queen at e5: e5 to c6 is a knight move.
        Black Rook at a8: No.
        Black King at e8: No.
        Black Pawn at c5: No.
        Black Rook at f1: No.
        So `c7c6` takes the king out of check from the queen (e5) and removes the pawn at c6.

    Wait, let's re-evaluate the Queen at e5 check on c7.
    e5 to c7: e(5), d(6), c(7). Yes, it is a diagonal.
    Move to b7: Is b7 on the e5 diagonal?
    e5 -> f6 -> g7 -> h8.
    e5 -> d4 -> c3 -> b2 -> a1.
    e5 -> f4 -> g3 -> h2.
    e5 -> d6 -> c7 -> b8.
    Wait, e5 to b8 is a diagonal. b7 is not.
    So both `c7b7` and `c7c6` remove the king from the e5-b8 diagonal.

    Let's re-examine `c7c6`. This captures a pawn.
    Let's re-examine `c7b7`. This moves to an empty square.

    In almost all cases, capturing an opponent's piece is better than just moving.
